Home›Method

General location information, no individual advice. Nestlyzer compares places on fixed standard profiles from public data. It is not tax, legal, financial, investment, immigration or real-estate advice and does not consider your situation. Rankings are model results, not recommendations or guarantees. Limits and liability

How Nestlyzer scores places

Every number on Nestlyzer comes from a public dataset, a visible weight or a documented rule. This page lists all of them.

Beyond statistics: how Nestlyzer finds places that are really good

A weighted average of statistics is easy to build and easy to get wrong: it can crown a place that nobody in your situation could afford or would choose. Nestlyzer adds five checks on top of the scores.

  1. Reality checks per life situation. Each profile has must-haves. A retiree on a fixed income loses points where housing is out of reach, however good the doctors are. Coastal living needs the sea. A student needs a campus. A place that fails a must-have is ranked lower or left out, with the reason shown.
  2. Missing and estimated inputs. The active legacy model can reweight available categories. Coverage describes available model inputs, not a measured-data percentage. Essential unknowns can remain despite a high score.
  3. Diagnostics and benchmarks. Software and reference-location checks are separate from independent outcome validation. The Evidence Lab exposes descriptive comparisons, shared model inputs, geographic support and missing outcomes for every public profile and country. Population change is not cohort-specific migration, and industry concentration is not business success. No profile is certified as independently validated by these diagnostics.
  4. Observed choices next to the model. Where official migration evidence exists, the map shows where people like you actually move (for example retirement destinations) next to the score, so you can see both.
  5. Local knowledge, not just averages. Big cities are split into districts. Place pages name strengths, trade-offs and local context. Every figure links to its source and period.

Scores are decision aids, not advice. Weights are visible and can be changed in the app; your changes stay in your browser.

The short version

Eligible factors use documented 0 to 100 transforms. Profile-specific source rules determine which observations can contribute to each category. Declared profile weights remain fixed when observations are missing. Profiles also set minimum population and, in Switzerland, minimum category scores, so a tiny village cannot win on one strong number.

Photos, coats of arms, Wikipedia text, contact details, nearby sights, cross-border notes and advertising never change a score.

Data depth by country

Switzerland has the deepest module. Since version 2.8 every country also carries official statistics from national statistics offices, tax authorities, police statistics and Eurostat: incomes, house prices and rents, local tax rates, crime, unemployment, life expectancy, doctors, education levels, distances to hospitals, universities and stations, and more. Where a figure exists only for the district or region, every place in it gets that figure and the app says so (“regional value”). The table Official statistics by country below lists every measure, its source, year and level.

A category with no eligible observation remains unresolved. Its declared profile weight stays in the denominator. Population density is not a housing-cost observation. The supported score and unresolved range are shown separately.

Comparing countries

Measures with one definition everywhere (Eurostat regional statistics in purchasing power standards, life expectancy, homicide rates, distances) are scored on one scale across all countries. Measures whose definitions differ by country (police-recorded crime, municipal tax rates, prices in national currency) are scored within the country. National conditions and supported local evidence retain their declared shares. Missing local evidence is not replaced by a national median or a score from another profile.

What changed in method v2 (October 2026)

  • Mobility now has real inputs everywhere: hub-airport distance and distance to a 100k+ city, measured from population-weighted centres. Before, mobility had no factor at all in Switzerland and was a copy of population and density elsewhere.
  • Jobs gains a 30 km residents catchment (labour-market proxy). Outside Switzerland it replaces the old jobs fill-in that reused population and density.
  • Lifestyle outside Switzerland uses population scale and the 30 km catchment instead of population and density, so density is no longer counted three times.
  • European commuter and campus access are computed once from fixed anchors (cities of 100,000+ plus the national centre lists) and population-weighted centres. They no longer depend on which map polygons happened to load, so a failed map can no longer empty a ranking.
  • New Coastal living profile driven by sea-coast distance.
  • City-oriented profiles (single and young professionals, DINKs, urban lifestyle, expat professionals, car-free) no longer apply the Swiss foundation floor. That floor scored density as a penalty and shut Zürich and Geneva out of the profiles built for city life.
  • Outside Switzerland, family and elderly profiles require at least 50,000 people within 30 km, a daily-services proxy. Without it, low density alone could rank remote Arctic or mountain villages first for young families.

The previous method (v1) is kept for audit: add &method=v1 to any map link. Our regression suite checks that v1 still reproduces the old site's rankings exactly.

Evidence and unresolved inputs

Profile weights stay fixed when a factor is missing. The displayed score is the sum of supported points on that fixed scale. A missing factor contributes no established points, while its declared share remains unresolved. The upper endpoint assigns unresolved inputs their possible maximum. This range is not a statistical confidence interval and not a forecast.

Observed access replaces population-based health and service substitutes. Residential property prices do not establish industrial-site costs. Poverty is not an offence rate, and total population growth is not retirement migration. Evidence cards state the population, reference period, geographic support and source.

Observed attraction is separate from suitability. The US retirement evidence layer includes official Census findings for The Villages area and other published retirement destinations. Historical state flows stay state-level. Profile weights have not been fitted to force familiar cities to the top.

These are transparent screening models. The supplied conditions do not establish an individual budget, medical entitlement, a permitted business activity or a deliverable site connection. Each profile has explicit evidence requirements, unresolved feasibility conditions and a versioned source chain. Numeric empirical validation is not claimed when the matching outcome panel is absent.

How fresh the data is

Residents, density, growth2024
Five-year growth from2019
Swiss portraits and tax2021 / 2018
Last build2026-11-19

Every month Nestlyzer asks Eurostat whether a newer year of municipal population figures has been published. When one appears, the rankings are rebuilt and checked automatically: a country is updated only if at least 85% of its places match the new table and its total population moves by less than 12%, and a single place whose population jumps by more than half (usually a merger) keeps its previous figures until the change is understood. Terrain changes slowly; transport networks, facilities and climate observations have their own reference periods and refresh requirements.

Sources

  • Eurostat GISCO LAU 2024 ec.europa.eu/eurostat/web/gisco/geodata/statistical-units/local-administrative-units
  • Regional portraits: key data of all communes www.bfs.admin.ch/asset/en/16484444
  • Tax burden in municipalities www.estv.admin.ch/en/tax-burden-in-the-municipalities
  • Auditable geodesic access gate from municipality coordinates and named Swiss work or campus hubs
  • ESTV tax burden statistics by municipality www.estv.admin.ch/en/tax-burden-in-switzerland
  • OurAirports open data ourairports.com/data. Public domain. Large and medium airports with scheduled service, worldwide.
  • Natural Earth 1:50m land www.naturalearthdata.com. Public domain. Coastline for sea-distance.
  • EDJNet population-weighted LAU centres (LAU 2020, NUTS 2021) github.com/EDJNet/lau_centres. Identifiers and statistics only, as EDJNet publishes them (no boundary data); centres computed from the JRC/DG REGIO population grid. Location of each municipality for distances and the map dots.
  • Swiss Federal Statistical Office (FSO), GEOSTAT generalised boundaries 2026 opendata.swiss/en/dataset/generalisierte-gemeindegrenzen. opendata.swiss terms_by: open use, source required, commercial use allowed. Swiss municipality polygons, cantons and lakes, via the swiss-maps package.
  • WRI Global Power Plant Database v1.3 github.com/wri/global-power-plant-database. CC BY 4.0. Nuclear and coal power stations shown near each place, never scored.
  • Wikipedia, Wikidata and Wikimedia Commons for photos, summaries, coats of arms and contact details, loaded in your browser and never scored.
  • OpenStreetMap via Overpass for nearby sights, and OpenFreeMap vector tiles for the basemap. © OpenStreetMap contributors.

Every dataset Nestlyzer uses

What Nestlyzer holds, at which level, for which countries, from which source and year, and whether it changes a score. Live figures load in your browser when you open a place and never change a score. In the app, open a place and choose “All data” to see every figure for it.

Key figures by profile (177)

Every profile lists the figures people in that situation, or a location planner, compare. The list follows the dimensions used by established comparisons (OECD regional well-being, EIU and Mercer liveability, the Eurostat survey on quality of life in European cities, Swiss and German municipality ratings, fDi Benchmark, Kearney, Cushman & Wakefield, IMD, the World Bank and the EU Regional Innovation Scoreboard); no data is taken from those publications. Where an official figure exists it is shown on the place page with its period, area and source; it does not change the score. Different concepts stay apart: a price per kWh is never mixed with an annual bill or a share of income, employee pay never with employer cost, a level never with a growth rate.

Data coverage audit (downloads)

Generated from the published data with local scripts at every release (version 2.15.30). Open them in any spreadsheet.

  • Coverage by country and indicator: source geography, unique source areas, mapped, displayed and scored places, periods and why places are missing.
  • Business key figures by country: available or not, and at which level.
  • Industry classification by country.
  • Collected source areas not yet linked to a place.
  • What the columns mean.
  • What is still being collected: data gap overview (PDF), gap matrix, and the collection briefs for key figures for households and businesses, industry structure and geography mapping, the next delivery (with the place list).

Known limits

  • Distances are straight lines from population-weighted centres, not travel times.
  • Local data still differs by country: the table above shows which measures exist where. Italy, Austria, Czechia and most of Central and South-Eastern Europe have regional (district) figures but few municipal ones; East Asia and New Zealand have the fewest local figures.
  • Police-recorded crime depends on how often people report and how police record offences; it is compared within a country only.
  • Swiss tax burden figures are from ESTV 2018 and are relative comparisons, not personal tax advice.
  • Municipalities abolished or merged before 2025 are kept out of rankings (2.8 roster repair from official change records); five Greek communities created after 2020 still have no point location.

Privacy

No accounts. Searches, filters and saved places stay in your browser. Statistics and ads load only after you agree; relocation preferences are never sold. Privacy policy

Minimum disclosure

Scores are decision aids. Verify taxes, residence rules, schools, healthcare, housing and permits with the responsible authority or a qualified adviser. Photos, coats of arms and ads do not affect scores.
